        else if (clean.tx.some(t => !t.name) || new Set(clean.tx.map(t => t.name)).size !== clean.tx.length || new Set(clean.comm.map(c => c.name)).size !== clean.comm.length || new Set(clean.doc.map(d => d.name)).size !== clean.doc.length) err = 'Names must be filled in and unique.';
        else if (clean.doc.some(d => d.basis === 'reporting_period' && !(d.threshold > 0))) err = 'A document type with a reporting-period basis needs a prescribed threshold of at least 1.';
        else {
          const keep = clean.doc.filter(d => d.active && d.basis === 'reporting_period').map(d => d.name);
          if (!keep.length) err = 'At least one active document type must keep a reporting-period basis, so regional reports still route to the designated staff.';
        }
      }
    } else if (sec === 'designations') {
      if (!Array.isArray(v) || v.length > 200) err = 'Invalid designations.'; else {
        clean = v.map(d => ({ name: str(d.name, 100), staff: str(d.staff, 40) }));
        if (clean.some(d => !d.name || !E.uById(d.staff) || !E.uById(d.staff).active) || new Set(clean.map(d => d.name)).size !== clean.length) err = 'Each region needs a unique name and an active designated staff member.';
      }
    } else if (sec === 'holidays') {
      if (!Array.isArray(v) || v.length > 400) err = 'Invalid holidays.'; else {
        clean = v.map(h => ({ date: str(h.date, 10), name: str(h.name, 120) }));
        if (clean.some(h => !/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(h.date) || !h.name)) err = 'Each holiday needs a date and a name.';
      }
    } else if (sec === 'settings') {
      if (!v) err = 'Invalid settings.'; else {
        const n = k => Math.max(0, Math.min(1000, parseInt(v[k], 10) || 0));
        clean = { orgName: str(v.orgName, 150), prefix: str(v.prefix, 12).toUpperCase().replace(/[^A-Z0-9]/g, ''), dueSoonDays: n('dueSoonDays'), adminDeskDays: n('adminDeskDays'), focalDeskDays: n('focalDeskDays'), idleMinutes: n('idleMinutes'), resubmit: v.resubmit === 'restart' ? 'restart' : 'returner' };
        if (!clean.orgName || !clean.prefix) err = 'The office name and prefix cannot be empty.';
      }
    }
    if (err) return fail(res, 400, err, 'invalid');
    s[sec] = clean; saveKV(sec); if (sec === 'holidays') E.rebuildHS();
    audit(u.id, 'Configuration changed: ' + sec, str(b.note, 300)); flushAudit(); bump();
    return send(res, 200, { ok: true });
  }

  /* --- audit, backup --- */
  if (m === 'GET' && p === '/api/audit') {
    if (!has(u, 'audit.view')) return fail(res, 403, 'Not allowed.', 'forbidden');
    const q = str(url.searchParams.get('q') || '', 100).toLowerCase();
    const rows = db.prepare('SELECT at,by,action,detail FROM audit ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT 3000').all().map(r => ({ at: r.at, by: r.by ? ((E.uById(r.by) || {}).name || r.by) : '', action: r.action, detail: r.detail }));
    return send(res, 200, { ok: true, rows: (q ? rows.filter(r => (r.by + ' ' + r.action + ' ' + r.detail).toLowerCase().includes(q)) : rows).slice(0, 200) });
  }
  if (m === 'GET' && p === '/api/admin/backup') {
    if (!has(u, 'data.manage')) return fail(res, 403, 'Not allowed.', 'forbidden');
    const s = S(), out = { format: 'dts-backup', version: 1, exportedAt: new Date().toISOString(), users: s.users, docs: s.docs, schedules: s.schedules, spConfigs: s.spConfigs, spItems: s.spItems,
      workflows:db.prepare('SELECT * FROM workflows').all(),workflowVersions:db.prepare('SELECT * FROM workflow_versions').all(),workflowNodes:db.prepare('SELECT * FROM workflow_nodes').all(),workflowEdges:db.prepare('SELECT * FROM workflow_edges').all(),workflowModes:db.prepare('SELECT * FROM workflow_modes').all(),workflowInstances:db.prepare('SELECT * FROM workflow_instances').all(),workflowHistory:db.prepare('SELECT * FROM workflow_history').all() };
    CONFIG_KEYS.forEach(k => { out[k] = s[k]; });
    out.audit = db.prepare('SELECT at,by,action,detail FROM audit ORDER BY id').all();
    audit(u.id, 'Backup exported', ''); flushAudit();
    return send(res, 200, out, { 'Content-Disposition': 'attachment; filename="dts-backup-' + new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10) + '.json"' });
  }
  if (m === 'POST' && p === '/api/admin/restore') {
    if (!has(u, 'data.manage')) return fail(res, 403, 'Not allowed.', 'forbidden');
    const b = await readBody(req, 100 * 1024 * 1024);
    if (b.format !== 'dts-backup' || !Array.isArray(b.users) || !Array.isArray(b.docs) || !b.settings || !b.refs || !b.perms || !Array.isArray(b.divisions)) return fail(res, 400, 'This is not a backup from this system.', 'invalid');
    if (!b.users.some(x => x.role === 'sysadmin' && x.active) || b.users.some(x => !x.id || typeof x.pw !== 'string')) return fail(res, 400, 'The backup must contain an active System Administrator.', 'invalid');
    tx(() => {
      db.exec('DELETE FROM users; DELETE FROM docs; DELETE FROM kv; DELETE FROM audit;');
      b.users.forEach(x => st.setUser.run(x.id, JSON.stringify(x)));
      b.docs.forEach(x => st.setDoc.run(x.id, JSON.stringify(x)));
      CONFIG_KEYS.forEach(k => { if (b[k] !== undefined) st.setKV.run(k, JSON.stringify(b[k])); });
      /* schedules are replaced only when the backup has them (backups made before the Personnel Schedule existed leave the current schedules alone) */
      if (Array.isArray(b.schedules)) { db.exec('DELETE FROM schedules'); b.schedules.map(normSchedule).filter(Boolean).forEach(x => st.setSched.run(x.id, JSON.stringify(x))); }
      /* Special Reports are replaced only when the backup has them (older backups leave the current ones alone) */
      if (Array.isArray(b.spConfigs) && Array.isArray(b.spItems)) {
        db.exec('DELETE FROM sr_assign; DELETE FROM sr_item; DELETE FROM sr_config');
        b.spConfigs.filter(x => x && x.id).forEach(srSaveCfg);
        b.spItems.filter(x => x && x.id && x.cfgId).forEach(it => { st.insSrItem.run(it.id, it.cfgId, it.year, it.month, srItemJson(it)); (it.assign || []).forEach(srSaveAssign); });
        st.setKV.run('spSeeded', '1');
      }
      /* Workflow tables are optional in older backups and remain untouched when absent. */
      if(Array.isArray(b.workflows)&&Array.isArray(b.workflowVersions)&&Array.isArray(b.workflowNodes)&&Array.isArray(b.workflowEdges)){
        db.exec('DELETE FROM workflow_history; DELETE FROM workflow_instances; DELETE FROM workflow_modes; DELETE FROM workflow_edges; DELETE FROM workflow_nodes; DELETE FROM workflow_versions; DELETE FROM workflows;');
        b.workflows.forEach(x=>db.prepare('INSERT INTO workflows(id,name,doc_type,created_at,created_by,active) VALUES(?,?,?,?,?,?)').run(x.id,x.name,x.doc_type,x.created_at,x.created_by,x.active));
        b.workflowVersions.forEach(x=>db.prepare('INSERT INTO workflow_versions(id,workflow_id,version,status,definition,created_at,created_by,published_at,published_by,validation) VALUES(?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?)').run(x.id,x.workflow_id,x.version,x.status,x.definition,x.created_at,x.created_by,x.published_at,x.published_by,x.validation||'[]'));
        b.workflowNodes.forEach(x=>db.prepare('INSERT INTO workflow_nodes(id,version_id,type,label,x,y,config) VALUES(?,?,?,?,?,?,?)').run(x.id,x.version_id,x.type,x.label,x.x,x.y,x.config));
        b.workflowEdges.forEach(x=>db.prepare('INSERT INTO workflow_edges(id,version_id,source,target,label,condition) VALUES(?,?,?,?,?,?)').run(x.id,x.version_id,x.source,x.target,x.label,x.condition));
        (b.workflowModes||[]).forEach(x=>db.prepare('INSERT INTO workflow_modes(doc_type,mode,version_id) VALUES(?,?,?)').run(x.doc_type,x.mode,x.version_id));
        (b.workflowInstances||[]).forEach(x=>db.prepare('INSERT INTO workflow_instances(id,workflow_id,version_id,document_id,status,current_node,created_at,updated_at) VALUES(?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?)').run(x.id,x.workflow_id,x.version_id,x.document_id,x.status,x.current_node,x.created_at,x.updated_at));
        (b.workflowHistory||[]).forEach(x=>db.prepare('INSERT INTO workflow_history(id,instance_id,at,by,action,detail) VALUES(?,?,?,?,?,?)').run(x.id,x.instance_id,x.at,x.by,x.action,x.detail));
      }
      (Array.isArray(b.audit) ? b.audit : []).forEach(a => st.addAudit.run(a.at, a.by || '', a.action, a.detail || ''));
    });
    loadAll();
    db.prepare('DELETE FROM sessions WHERE h<>?').run(sess.hash);
    if (!E.uById(u.id)) db.prepare('DELETE FROM sessions').run();
    audit(u.id, 'Backup restored', b.docs.length + ' documents, ' + b.users.length + ' users'); flushAudit(); bump();
    return send(res, 200, { ok: true });
  }
  if (m === 'POST' && p === '/api/admin/clear-docs') {
    if (!has(u, 'data.manage')) return fail(res, 403, 'Not allowed.', 'forbidden');
    S().docs.length = 0; S().seq = {}; db.exec('DELETE FROM docs'); saveKV('seq');
    audit(u.id, 'All documents deleted', ''); flushAudit(); bump();
    return send(res, 200, { ok: true });
  }
  return fail(res, 404, 'Not found.', 'not_found');
}

/* ---------- static files ---------- */
const STATIC = { '/': ['index.html', 'text/html; charset=utf-8'], '/index.html': ['index.html', 'text/html; charset=utf-8'], '/client.js': ['client.js', 'text/javascript; charset=utf-8'], '/engine.js': ['engine.js', 'text/javascript; charset=utf-8'], '/app.css': ['app.css', 'text/css; charset=utf-8'], '/schedule.js': ['schedule.js', 'text/javascript; charset=utf-8'], '/schedule.css': ['schedule.css', 'text/css; charset=utf-8'], '/special.js': ['special.js', 'text/javascript; charset=utf-8'], '/special.css': ['special.css', 'text/css; charset=utf-8'], '/workflow.js': ['workflow.js', 'text/javascript; charset=utf-8'], '/workflow.css': ['workflow.css', 'text/css; charset=utf-8'], '/favicon.svg': ['favicon.svg', 'image/svg+xml'], '/favicon.png': ['favicon.png', 'image/png'], '/logo-black.png': ['logo-black.png', 'image/png'], '/logo-white.png': ['logo-white.png', 'image/png'], '/login-bg.png': ['login-bg.png', 'image/png'] };
const CSP = "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://fonts.googleapis.com; font-src https://fonts.gstatic.com; img-src 'self' data:; connect-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'";
const server = http.createServer(async (req, res) => {
  res.setHeader('X-Content-Type-Options', 'nosniff');
  res.setHeader('Referrer-Policy', 'no-referrer');
  res.setHeader('X-Frame-Options', 'DENY');
  res.setHeader('Content-Security-Policy', CSP);
  if (isHttps(req)) res.setHeader('Strict-Transport-Security', 'max-age=31536000');
  try {
    const url = new URL(req.url, 'http://x');
    if (url.pathname === '/healthz') return send(res, 200, { ok: true });
    if (url.pathname.startsWith('/api/')) return await handleApi(req, res, url);
    if (req.method !== 'GET' && req.method !== 'HEAD') { res.writeHead(405); return res.end(); }
    const f = STATIC[url.pathname];
    if (!f) { res.writeHead(url.pathname === '/favicon.ico' ? 204 : 404); return res.end(); }
    const buf = fs.readFileSync(path.join(PUB, f[0]));
    res.writeHead(200, { 'Content-Type': f[1], 'Cache-Control': 'no-cache' });
    return res.end(buf);
  } catch (e) {
    if (!res.headersSent) {
      if (e.status) return fail(res, e.status, e.message, 'bad_request');
      console.error(e);
      return fail(res, 500, 'Something went wrong on the server.', 'server');
    }
    res.end();
  }
});

/* ---------- daily backups of the database file ---------- */
function backupDb() {
  try {
    const dir = path.join(DATA, 'backups'); fs.mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
    const f = path.join(dir, 'dts-' + new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10) + '.sqlite');
    if (!fs.existsSync(f)) db.exec("VACUUM INTO '" + f.replace(/'/g, "''") + "'");
    fs.readdirSync(dir).filter(n => n.endsWith('.sqlite')).sort().slice(0, -14).forEach(n => fs.unlinkSync(path.join(dir, n)));
  } catch (e) { console.error('Backup failed:', e.message); }
}

(async () => {
  if (db.prepare('SELECT COUNT(*) c FROM users').get().c === 0) await firstRun(); else loadAll();
  backupDb(); setInterval(backupDb, DAY).unref();
  /* Special Reports: recover anything missed while the server was down, then re-check every 15 minutes (a missed configured generation day is caught by the next check) */
  srTick(); setInterval(srTick, 15 * 60000).unref();
  server.listen(PORT, HOST, () => console.log('Document Tracking & Routing System listening on http://' + (HOST === '0.0.0.0' ? 'localhost' : HOST) + ':' + PORT + '  (time zone ' + process.env.TZ + ')'));
})();
process.on('SIGINT', () => { server.close(); db.close(); process.exit(0); });
process.on('SIGTERM', () => { server.close(); db.close(); process.exit(0); });

FILE: README.md
========================================
# Document Tracking & Routing System (multi-user)

A small web server plus a browser app. Everyone signs in with their own account; the server keeps
the documents in one SQLite database and checks every action, so what a person may do is enforced
on the server, not only hidden in the screen.

- No dependencies to install. Only **Node.js 22.13 or newer** is needed (it includes SQLite).
- All workflow rules live in `public/engine.js`; the server runs them, the browser only displays results.
- Other users' changes appear on your screen within about 15 seconds.

## Run it

    node server.js                 # http://localhost:3000

First run creates `data/dts.sqlite` and **`data/initial-credentials.txt`** with a random temporary
password for every person in your workflow document (administrator username: `admin`). Each person
must set their own password at first sign-in. Hand the details out, then delete that file.

Try it with sample documents and simple passwords (never for real use):

    SEED_DEMO=1 DATA_DIR=./demo-data node server.js     # passwords: Track@2026 (admin: Admin@2026)

## Settings (environment variables)

| Variable | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `PORT` / `HOST` | 3000 / 0.0.0.0 | Where to listen. Use `HOST=127.0.0.1` behind a proxy. |
| `DATA_DIR` | `./data` | Database, daily backups, credentials file. |
| `TZ` | `Asia/Manila` | Working-day counts and deadlines use this time zone. |
| `TRUST_PROXY=1` | off | Read the client address and HTTPS status from the reverse proxy. |
| `COOKIE_SECURE=1` | off | Mark the session cookie Secure (always set this with HTTPS). |
| `SEED_DEMO=1` | off | First run only: add sample documents and simple passwords. |

## Startup screen

Opening the page shows the POSSD splash ("System is loading...") straight away. It leaves, with a short fade, as soon
as the server has answered the first request (it fades in and out over about one second each): signed in goes to the dashboard, signed out goes to the sign-in page.
There is no fixed delay. If the server cannot be reached, answers with an error, or does not answer within 15 seconds,
the splash shows "Unable to start the application." with a **Retry** button (no technical details are shown; the browser
console keeps them). It uses no internet resources. `start-dts.bat` is unchanged: it still waits for `/healthz`, then opens the page.

## Put it on a server (HTTPS is required for real use)

Passwords travel from the browser to the server, so serve it over HTTPS.

1. Copy this folder to the server (for example `/opt/dts`) and install Node 22.13+.
2. Put an HTTPS reverse proxy in front. With Caddy, `Caddyfile.example` is all you need
   (certificates are automatic). nginx or IIS work too.
3. Run it as a service: `dts.service` is a ready systemd unit. Or use `docker compose up -d`.
4. Open the address, sign in as `admin` with the temporary password, set your own.

## Backups

- The server copies the database to `DATA_DIR/backups/` daily and keeps 14 days.
- Settings & data (administrator) can export or restore a full JSON backup.
- Also copy `DATA_DIR` off the machine regularly.

## Who is who (set up from your workflow)

- CMED: focal Aubrey Camille C. Cabrera, manager Myles Rovi P. Martinez
- CMSD: focal Mary Flor F. Aquino, manager Judy F. Villarete
- Admin: focal Judy Ann T. Pacaanas
- Department: focal Oscar B. Hanova Jr.
- Anyone named as a division's focal gets the focal abilities (assign, review, see the division), whatever their role.
- The Admin and Department divisions have no Division Manager, so any Division Manager may do the division review for them.
- Change all of this later under Users and Divisions & focals.

## Deadlines (set them in Reference list)

Every deadline comes from the Document Type, so nothing is hard-coded in the app. Under
**Reference list → Document Types** the administrator sets, per document type:

- **Prescribed threshold** - how many working days (or calendar days) are allowed.
- **Unit / basis** - `working days`, `calendar days`, or `nth working day of the month after the
  reporting period` (used by Consolidated CMR and PPR).
- **Active / inactive** - an inactive type stays valid on old records but cannot be chosen for new ones.

One function in `public/engine.js` (`computeDeadline`) works out every deadline for the whole system:
logging, editing, the live form preview and the displayed status. Working-day counts skip Saturdays,
Sundays and the holidays listed under Reference list → Holidays.

**Consolidated CMR and PPR** uses **Reporting period** (the month the report covers) instead of a
division. The deadline is the **6th working day of the month right after** the reporting period.
Type `Sep`, `september` or `9` and it settles on `September`; anything else is refused, and the
deadline cannot be typed over by hand.

## Special Reports (mandatory recurring reports, created by the system)

**Reference lists → Special Reports** (needs the configuration permission). Users never log these by hand:
the server creates each report when it falls due, gives it a deadline and assigns it.

- **Schedules** tab: name, frequency (Monthly / Quarterly / Specific months / Annual), the exact reporting
  months, generation day in the following month, deadline threshold, deadline basis (working days of the month after the reporting month),
  who is responsible, active or inactive. Nothing is hard-coded; edit any of it without touching code.
- Starting schedules: Summary of CMR (monthly, 25), Summary of PPR (monthly, 20), List of Terminated
  (March, June, September, December; 17), PPA to COA (January, March, June, September, December; 17),
  CMR and PPR Tracking (monthly, 15).
  **They start inactive with nobody assigned.** Assign the responsible people and activate each one.
- **Generation:** on the configured day of the month after the reporting month (default 1; a day beyond that month's
  length uses its last day), e.g. "Summary of CMR as of January 2026" appears in February 2026. The server checks on start-up and every 15 minutes, so a missed generation date is made up
  on the next check (up to 24 months back, never before the schedule's first period). Repeats never duplicate:
  the database allows one report per schedule + year + month and one assignment per person per report.
- **Deadline:** the n-th working day counted from the 1st of the month after the reporting month, using the same
  working-day rules and holiday list as every other deadline. If n is larger than the month has working days
  (25 in February 2026, which has 20), the count carries on into the next month. Add each year's holidays
  under Reference list → Holidays; a warning shows when the current or next year has none.
- **Review workflow:** the assigned staff member submits the report to a Supervisor, then it moves through Division Manager and Department Manager review, clearance, and log out. Reviewers can return it for compliance; remarks are kept in its movement history.
- **For My Action / Dashboard:** assigned people and the current reviewer see the report in For My Action (and in its count). Generated
  reports appear in the existing dashboard registry with a Special Report badge. Logging one out removes it from the action list; the record stays.
- **Reassign** a pending report from the Generated reports tab; the report is the same, the old assignment is
  kept as history. Changing a schedule affects only reports generated afterwards. The Regular reports page has
  Consolidated CMR and PPR and Special Report tabs; analytics and graphs include generated reports by generation date.
  Special Reports do not create Drive links or Personnel Schedule events.
- Included in the JSON backup/restore and in the Audit log. The System Administrator account has no For My Action
  list, so it cannot be assigned a report.

## Personnel Schedule (inspections, leave and meetings)

A calendar (Month / Week / Day / Agenda) showing who is on inspection, on leave or in a meeting.
Everyone signed in can view it; adding, editing and deleting needs the **Add, edit and delete
inspections, leave and meetings** permission (System Administrator has it; grant it to other roles
under Roles & permissions).

- People are the existing user accounts; there is no second personnel list. One inspection or
  meeting is one record with several people assigned.
- Leave types are configured under **Reference lists → Leave types** (needs the configuration permission).
- Overlaps (same person, overlapping dates and times; leave counts as the whole day) show a warning.
  An authorised user can review it and choose **Save anyway**. Nothing is blocked silently.
- Schedules are included in the JSON backup and restore. Changes appear in the Audit log.
- Projects: the application has no project records yet, so an inspection's project is typed in
  (suggestions come from projects already used on earlier inspections).

## Security notes

- Passwords: scrypt with a random salt. Sessions: random token in an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict cookie; the server
  stores only its hash. Idle timeout is set in Settings (default 30 minutes).
- Five wrong passwords lock that username and address out for 15 minutes.
- Requests must carry a custom header (CSRF), and the page uses a strict Content-Security-Policy.
- Deactivating a user or resetting their password ends their sessions at once.
- Everything is audited (Audit log page).

## Limits

- One server process holds the data in memory and writes to SQLite. That comfortably suits an office,
  but do not run several copies against the same database.
- No email, so password resets are done by the administrator.

## Tests

    SEED_DEMO=1 DATA_DIR=/tmp/t PORT=3111 node server.js &   # then, each suite in turn:
    PORT=3111 node test/api.test.js
    PORT=3111 node test/new-rules.test.js
    PORT=3111 node test/rules4.test.js
    PORT=3111 node test/routing-visibility.test.js
    PORT=3111 node test/schedule.test.js
    PORT=3111 node test/schedule.attack.test.js        # tries to break the schedule API
    # Special Reports need a server whose clock the tests may set (SR_TEST is for testing only):
    SEED_DEMO=1 SR_TEST=1 SR_NOW=2026-01-15 DATA_DIR=/tmp/t PORT=3111 node server.js &
    PORT=3111 node test/phase11.test.js
    PORT=3111 node test/phase11.ui.test.js             # browser check (Playwright)
    # the two browser suites need Playwright + Chromium (npm i playwright):
    node test/schedule.ui.test.js
    node test/schedule.attack.ui.test.js

Run them against a fresh `DATA_DIR` each time: the suites change reference lists and documents, so
re-running them on the same database can fail on leftover state rather than on real problems.

FILE: WORKFLOW_ENGINE.md
========================================
# Workflow Engine implementation and migration status

## Current application

- Node.js 22.13+ using `node:http`, built-in `node:sqlite`, and static vanilla JavaScript, CSS, and HTML. There is no React app, PostgreSQL service, or ORM.
- `public/engine.js` owns the existing workflow rules and permissions. `server.js` authenticates users and applies document actions. The document status sequence is `received`, `at_focal`, `processing`, `compliance`, `sup_review`, `div_review`, `dept_review`, `cleared`, `return_out`, and `out`.
- Existing rules include focal grants, role permissions, override, designated OIC, and approver fallback through `deptApprovers` (present Department Manager, present Division Manager, nominated OIC). Existing action history remains embedded in document records and in the audit table.

## Added foundation

- Additive SQLite tables store workflows, versions, nodes, transitions, document type mode, execution instance, and instance history. Existing document tables and JSON records are not rewritten.
- Definitions are editable as drafts; published versions are immutable and can be forked into a new draft. The visual editor uses the existing application styles and browser APIs; no package or service was added.
- System Administrator only can access the workflow page and its APIs. The converted existing workflow is a draft with the current review sequence and a return path. Validation catches missing/duplicate start, missing finish, unreachable nodes, dead ends, non-terminating paths, invalid responsibility references, malformed conditions, and invalid decision branches.
- Simulation accepts an optional document ID and only returns a simulated path; it does not write to document state.
- Workflow data is included in newly exported JSON backups. Older backups without workflow fields leave workflow tables untouched when restored.

## Production behavior and migration boundary

Every document type remains `LEGACY`. The current document creation and action handlers were not changed. The `ENGINE` mode is intentionally rejected by the server because document creation, task assignment, permission-aware actions, and status/history projection have not yet been connected to workflow instances. Do not switch production document types until that integration and scenario comparison are complete. Existing documents are not attached to a new version.

This implementation is Phases 1–3 groundwork. It does not yet fulfill production cutover, runtime task execution, or Phase 4 verification. The `workflow_instances` and `workflow_history` tables are reserved for that integration; this release does not write operational instances there.

## OIC and responsibility reuse

The draft records the current manager/OIC fallback and does not add a second OIC configuration. The persisted definition format and validator recognize role, user, division, and role-plus-division responsibility references; the properties panel exposes role, specific user, division, role-plus-division, and current assignee configuration. Read-only simulation resolves candidates; operational task responsibility still needs runtime integration with the existing permission/responsibility rules.

## Capacity and infrastructure

The README describes a single-server office deployment. For that stated usage, the existing Node/SQLite setup is sufficient: graph payloads are capped at 100 nodes and 250 edges, lookup indexes are added, and no polling, background worker, dependency, or external storage is introduced. SQLite WAL and the existing daily database backups remain in use. No major infrastructure increase is needed for definitions and administration. Actual user, document, and execution volumes were not supplied, so larger-scale capacity cannot be certified from repository evidence alone.

## Modified files

- `server.js`: additive schema, protected workflow APIs, validation, simulation, audit, and backup/restore support; no change to current document action logic.
- `public/client.js`: System Administrator navigation and route gate.
- `public/index.html`: workflow assets.
- `public/workflow.js`, `public/workflow.css`: designer, conversion, validation, simulation, version controls, and status display.

## Untouched areas

`public/engine.js`, existing document and Special Report action handlers, user/role/division/OIC data structures, existing document statuses and histories, authentication, and deployment configuration were left unchanged. No package dependency or destructive migration was added.

## Remaining migration work

Implement runtime responsibility resolution and task execution by reusing the current authorization rules; support full condition editing and branch selection; bind new-document creation to a pinned published version; write instance/history rows transactionally alongside legacy-compatible document history; build document-specific simulation scenarios; then compare normal, return/re-review, OIC, manager fallback, override, and clear-for-out behavior for one document type before enabling it. Existing documents must retain their legacy path or an explicitly selected pinned version.

## Designer revision — 7 October 2026

The palette is simplified to Start, Action, Decision and End. An Action defaults to Review and its Action type property chooses Review, Approval, Assignment, Notification, Return/Revision or Clear for Out. The stored type remains the existing supported business type, preserving old workflows and validation/simulation behavior. This does not introduce operational task execution. Connector text now uses the application's actual `--ink` theme token rather than the undefined `--text` token. Return/backward connections route below the nodes and enter the destination's right-side return input; connection properties can explicitly choose left, right or automatic routing. Target-side metadata persists with the connection.

The reference-style diagram update adds a dotted canvas, pastel/slate/minimal themes, diamond decision nodes, rounded terminal nodes, multiline descriptions and right-angle connectors. Layout has hierarchical and adaptive (predecessor-position ordering) modes with left-to-right, right-to-left, top-to-bottom and bottom-to-top directions. Layout runs only when Arrange is selected. Hand mode complements space/middle-button panning. Discard changes confirms and reloads the last saved draft; it does not delete the workflow/version. Browser checks cover all four directions, theme switching, and restoration of saved positions/connections after discarding.

The designer now provides compact workflow/version selectors, a searchable categorized palette, palette drag/drop, shared node content and visual shells, dedicated input/output handles, branch labels, live preview, connection properties and controlled condition fields. START rejects incoming edges, END rejects outgoing edges, self/duplicate handle connections are rejected, and approval/decision outputs persist in edge condition metadata.

Multi-selection, group movement, delete, duplicate, copy/paste, undo/redo, keyboard shortcuts, wheel zoom, space/middle-button pan, calculated fit, grid snapping, minimap and explicit auto layout are implemented. Canvas edits do not send requests for each movement. Save status and unsaved-change protection are visible. Graph fingerprints reject stale saves from a second administrator. Published versions can be validated/simulated without attempting to save them; publishing requires a confirmation dialog.

Simulation now evaluates the supported controlled edge conditions against a selected document, reports responsible user candidates and branch results, calls the existing manager/OIC resolver for department approval responsibility, supports selected branch labels, and bounds revision cycles. This is a read-only trace, not proof of authorization or business-rule equivalence. It does not execute assignments, approvals, notifications or document status transitions.

Remaining master-prompt scope includes executable generic nodes, delay/parallel/merge semantics, activation/archive administration, runtime task/instance APIs, full legacy equivalence and migration verification, context menus and canvas notes. A node registry alone does not make those execution features complete. No production document type was migrated.

Tests use a fresh temporary demo database. The existing API regression suite passed 51 checks. Workflow API checks cover persistence, branch metadata, conditions, responsibility candidates, immutable versions, conflicts, authorization and read-only simulation. Browser checks passed for palette drop, port-drag preview and creation, live labels, saving, undo/redo, duplicate/delete, fit, minimap, and absence of page errors. The tests are in `test/workflow.ui.test.js`; run with Playwright and Chromium or `WF_BROWSER_CHANNEL=msedge` when Edge is installed.

FILE: ANALYSIS-2026-10-15.md

# Project analysis — 15 October 2026

## Scope and result

Reviewed application source, deployment files, documentation, and test infrastructure. Ran the nine default API regression suites using fresh demo databases, plus focused probes for restore validation and concurrent user creation. No application source or existing `data` records were changed. Browser suites were not rerun. This is a focused code and behavior review, not a comprehensive security or production capacity assessment.

All nine regression suites passed. Eight suites reported **477 checks passed, zero failed**; the workflow suite also passed its assertions without a numerical summary. Application JavaScript syntax checks passed.

## Architecture and file map

| File or directory | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `server.js` | HTTP server, authentication, SQLite persistence, API validation, recurring report generation, workflow administration, backups and restore. |
| `public/engine.js` | Shared business rules: permissions, routing, deadlines, manager/OIC selection, schedule conflicts and recurring report helpers. The server loads it through a VM context. |
| `public/client.js` | Main browser interface: sign-in, registry, document actions, analytics, reporting and administration. |
| `public/schedule.js` | Personnel calendar and event editing. |
| `public/special.js` | Recurring report interface and review actions. |
| `public/workflow.js` | Visual workflow designer, versions, validation and simulation. |
| `public/*.css`, `public/index.html` | Presentation, application shell and startup splash. |
| `data/` | Existing SQLite database, WAL files, initial credential file and database backups. Treat these as sensitive application data. |
| `test/` | API and browser regressions, isolated runner, synthetic data seeders and load benchmarks. |
| `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose.yml`, `dts.service`, `Caddyfile.example` | Container and Linux service/reverse proxy deployment options. |

The application uses Node.js built-ins and SQLite, with vanilla browser JavaScript and no package dependencies. Node 24.21.0 was available during this review. Most operational records are stored as JSON in SQLite and loaded into process memory. Run a single application process per database, as the README specifies.

## Findings, in priority order

### 1. High — malformed restore data can be committed before validation fails

**Location:** `server.js:971–1008`, particularly the initial checks at line 975 and `loadAll()` at line 1004; `public/engine.js:99`.

Restore checks a few top-level fields, replaces persisted records inside a transaction, commits, and then loads the replacement state. It does not fully validate the nested configuration before committing.

**Confirmed reproduction:** Exported an isolated demo backup, changed only `holidays` to `[null]`, and restored it. The endpoint returned HTTP 500 when `rebuildHS()` read `h.date`, but a separate read-only SQLite connection confirmed that `[null]` was already persisted. Startup calls the same loader, so the invalid data can also prevent a later restart from completing until the database is repaired or replaced.

**Recommended change:** Validate all backup structures and references, and construct a valid replacement state before replacing persisted records. Invalid input must leave the previous database and in-memory state intact. Preserve a recovery backup before applying a restore.

### 2. Medium — concurrent requests can create duplicate usernames

**Location:** `server.js:845–848`; users table definition at line 32.

The uniqueness check runs before awaiting password hashing. Two requests can both pass the check before either adds the new user. The database only constrains the user ID, not the username stored inside the JSON.

**Confirmed reproduction:** Submitted two simultaneous user creation requests with the same username. Both returned HTTP 200, and the state contained two users with that username. Login selects the first matching account, making access ambiguous.

**Recommended change:** Enforce case-insensitive username uniqueness in SQLite, with an appropriate migration for existing duplicates, and map constraint conflicts to a clear client error. Rechecking after hashing can close the immediate race in the current single-process design, but a database constraint is the stronger invariant.

### 3. Medium — CSV exports preserve formula prefixes

**Location:** `public/client.js:89–94`, with user-entered document fields exported at line 572.

The CSV helper escapes separators, quotes and newlines, but leaves values beginning with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` unchanged. User-entered subjects, sources and reference numbers can therefore reach spreadsheet software as formula-like cells.

This finding is based on source inspection; opening exports in spreadsheet applications was outside this review.

**Recommended change:** Apply a spreadsheet-safe text policy to exported user-controlled cells, including formula-prefix handling. Preserve legitimate numeric fields deliberately.

## Product and maintenance observations

- **Workflow execution remains incomplete by design.** Workflow definitions can be edited, published, forked and simulated, but `server.js:615` rejects `ENGINE` activation. Live documents still follow the legacy engine. Published diagrams do not execute assignments, approvals, notifications or status transitions. `WORKFLOW_ENGINE.md` accurately documents this boundary.
- **All authenticated users can read all ordinary documents.** `public/engine.js:266` returns true for any signed-in user, and `/api/state` uses that function. Action permissions remain separate. This is an observable access policy, not an assumed defect; any need for division-level confidentiality would require server-side changes.
- **Full-state synchronization is the principal scaling cost.** `/api/state` sends all visible documents and their histories; registry pagination is performed in the browser. Prior stress reports describe this limitation, but their October 7 performance measurements were not rerun here.
- **Large modules increase change risk.** The main browser module is about 162 KB and the server about 93 KB. Extracting authentication, persistence and API domains into modules would make future runtime workflow integration easier to review.
- **Default test coverage is easy to miss.** `npm test` runs only `api.test.js` against a separately started server. The isolated runner is the more complete entry point: `node test/run-regression.js`.
- **This directory is not a Git checkout.** Git status could not establish a baseline or identify earlier local modifications.

## Verification evidence

Regression results and per-suite logs:

`.test-runs/2026-10-15T00-15-43-219Z/results.json`

| Suite | Result |
| --- | --- |
| api | 51 passed |
| new-rules | 12 passed |
| rules4 | 56 passed |
| routing-visibility | 26 passed |
| schedule | 76 passed |
| schedule.attack | 98 passed |
| phase10 | 30 passed |
| phase11 | 128 passed |
| workflow | Passed |

Focused reproduction evidence:

`.test-runs/analysis-probes-20261015-1792023425687/probe-results.json`

The malformed database used for the restore probe is confined to that isolated directory. It is intentionally invalid test evidence and should not be used as an application database.

FILE: FULL-FEATURE-AUDIT-2026-10-15.md
========================================
# Full DTS roadmap audit — 15 October 2026

The current app covers the ordinary registration/routing/review/deadline/reporting cycle. The roadmap also describes a file repository, records management system, event notification system, generic task engine and configurable workflow runtime; those broader capabilities are incomplete or absent.

## Scope and interpretation

Every bullet requirement in catalog sections A–W is assessed below. Section V's five numbered capabilities and six future possibilities are included as eleven rows. Proposed roles, lifecycle guidance, dashboard examples, navigation, seven distinguishing features, rollout phases, architecture and the repeated final 25-item checklist are assessed after the catalog. Repeated requirements remain separate rows, so the totals are a requirement inventory, not a percentage of unique features completed.

| Status | Meaning | Rows |
| --- | --- | ---: |
| Available | Implemented in the current source, subject to the stated limits. | 104 |
| Equivalent | Existing controls produce the basic requested outcome under a different name/form. | 40 |
| Partial | A related capability exists, but an explicit part of the requirement is absent or defective. | 170 |
| Missing | No corresponding app capability was found. | 122 |
| Unverified | Depends on deployment, host controls or policy evidence not available in source. | 3 |

Total catalog rows: **439**. Do not interpret this as production certification. This review inspected local source and tests; the earlier analysis in this chat ran nine API suites successfully (477 counted checks plus workflow assertions). Browser, remote-host, compliance, disaster-recovery and independent-user soak tests were not rerun for this checklist. No application code or existing application database was changed.

## Important boundaries and defects

1. The visual workflow designer has draft editing, published immutable versions, validation and simulation. It does not execute assignments/approvals/notifications or replace the live legacy workflow. ENGINE activation is rejected. Publishing is not production activation.
2. A URL field and revised-link counter are not stored attachments or historical file versions. Old URLs and per-file metadata are not retained.
3. All signed-in users can view ordinary documents. Role/division restrictions primarily control actions. There is no confidential-record viewing policy.
4. The bell counts actionable documents, not unread events. No event history, mail delivery, digests or escalation jobs exist.
5. CSV opens in Excel and browser printing can produce PDF. These are useful equivalents; native XLSX generation and server-side report PDF generation are absent.
6. Restore with holidays set to [null] returned HTTP 500 after invalid data was committed. Two concurrent create-user requests produced duplicate usernames. Both were confirmed in isolated demo databases during the earlier analysis.
7. Special Report display tracking code is SR-year-month, without the schedule ID. Different schedules for the same month can show the same code even though internal report IDs and schedule-period uniqueness remain separate.
8. Many operational permissions are enforced on the server, but report.print/export gates primarily control the browser UI. Users who can fetch the full state can extract its data independently. Confidential export/view restrictions are not implemented.
9. Monthly chart data is first restricted to records received in the selected period, then counts releases in that subset. A document received in August and released in September is omitted from September-only release totals. This was identified by source inspection, not a new runtime reproduction.
10. The age-band graph click handler applies only the ongoing filter, so clicking a particular age band opens all ongoing records rather than that exact band. Identified by source inspection.
11. Live document edits have no general optimistic concurrency check. Some document/sequence/audit writes are separate commits, unlike the transactional workflow graph/report paths. Full client state download is the main scaling cost.

Evidence: [server.js:615](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/server.js:615>), [public/engine.js:266](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/public/engine.js:266>), [public/engine.js:512](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/public/engine.js:512>), [public/special.js:78](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/public/special.js:78>), [public/client.js:167](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/public/client.js:167>), [public/client.js:676](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/public/client.js:676>), [public/client.js:768](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/public/client.js:768>), [ANALYSIS-2026-10-15.md:38](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/ANALYSIS-2026-10-15.md:38>).

| O9 | Secure attachment handling and file type validation. | Missing | No managed attachment processing/type validation. |
| O10 | File upload size limits. | Missing | JSON request size caps exist; they are not attachment upload size limits. |
| O11 | Protection against unauthorized direct file access. | Partial | Static serving uses an explicit allowlist; external file access is controlled by the external host, not DTS. |
| O12 | Restricted access to confidential records. | Missing | No confidential-record restrictions. |
| O13 | Automatic session timeout. | Available | Idle and absolute session expiration exist. |
| O14 | Security event logs. | Available | Successful/failed sign-ins and related security/admin events are logged. |
| O15 | Dependency updates and vulnerability checks. | Partial | No third-party app dependencies; no automated Node/container/browser vulnerability/update process is provided. |
| O16 | Data retention and authorized disposal policies. | Missing | No retention/disposal policy engine. |
| O17 | Privacy notice and appropriate personal-data handling. | Unverified | No privacy-notice screen found; agency personal-data policy/procedures were not supplied or assessed. |
| O18 | Incident response and recovery procedures. | Missing | No documented incident response or tested disaster recovery runbook. |

| R20 | Light and dark modes. | Available | Automatic/light/dark themes exist. |
| R21 | Custom agency logo and branding. | Partial | Agency image assets and configurable organization name exist; logo replacement is a file edit, not an upload setting. |
| R22 | Print-friendly views where operationally required. | Available | Routing slips, tables, reports, graphs and schedules have print views. |
| R23 | Excel export as the primary data extraction feature where preferred. | Equivalent | CSV is the extraction format and can be opened in Excel; native XLSX is absent. |

### W. Performance and technical reliability

Source evidence: [server.js:27](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/server.js:27>), [server.js:83](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/server.js:83>), [server.js:615](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/server.js:615>), [server.js:1030](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/server.js:1030>), [test/run-regression.js:1](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/test/run-regression.js:1>), [STRESS-TEST-1000-2026-10-07.md:65](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/STRESS-TEST-1000-2026-10-07.md:65>).

| ID | Roadmap requirement | Status | Current implementation / gap |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| W1 | Indexed database columns for common searches. | Partial | IDs and workflow/report lookup indexes exist; ordinary searchable document metadata is JSON and is searched in browser memory. |
| W2 | Efficient server-side filtering, sorting, and pagination. | Missing | Ordinary document filtering/sorting/pagination happen in the browser after downloading state. |
| W3 | Transactions for critical workflow operations. | Partial | Graph/report/restore operations use SQL transactions; ordinary document mutation, sequence and audit writes are not one combined transaction. |
| W4 | Prevention of duplicate submissions and conflicting updates. | Partial | Workflow drafts have revision conflict checks and recurring reports have unique keys; ordinary updates lack general revision/idempotency protection and user creation has a confirmed race. |
| W5 | Database constraints for important relationships. | Partial | Workflow foreign keys and recurring-report uniqueness exist; many user/document/division references inside JSON have no database constraints. |
| W6 | Background jobs for scheduled reports and bulk processing. | Partial | In-process timers generate recurring reports; no durable worker queue or bulk-processing job system. |
| W7 | Centralized error logging. | Partial | Console error logging exists; no centralized persistent error service/view. |
| W8 | Health checks and uptime monitoring. | Partial | healthz exists; no uptime monitor/alerts. |
| W9 | Graceful handling of interrupted uploads. | Missing | No resumable/interrupted upload handling; binary uploads are absent. |
| W10 | File size and storage usage monitoring. | Missing | No storage usage/file-size monitor. |
| W11 | Database migration versioning. | Partial | CREATE IF NOT EXISTS and compatibility upgrades exist; no numbered migration ledger. |
| W12 | Automated tests for workflow transitions and permissions. | Available | API regression tests cover document rules, roles, fallback/OIC, schedules and designer behavior. |
| W13 | Concurrent-user testing. | Partial | Concurrent requests are tested; independent simultaneous user-session soak testing is absent. |
| W14 | Backup restoration testing. | Partial | JSON restore round trips are tested; full snapshot disaster recovery and malformed restore rejection are incomplete. |
| W15 | Recovery procedures for failed deployments. | Missing | No failed-deployment rollback/recovery runbook. |
| W16 | Separation of development, testing, and production environments. | Partial | DATA_DIR and isolated test runner separate databases; no formal development/staging/production deployment configuration. |

## Proposed roles and policy notes

| Proposed role | Assessment |
| --- | --- |
| System Administrator | Available; broad override, settings, accounts, references, workflow designer and backups. |
| Admin Staff / Receiving Clerk | Available; intake/forwarding/release with configurable permissions. |
| Focal Person / Supervisor | Available as focal and supervisor roles, plus division-focal grants. Automatic escalation is missing. |
| Processing Staff | Available for assigned processing/submission and correction; file uploads are missing. |
| Division Manager / OIC | Available manager review and nominated OIC; timed authority/expiration are missing. |
| Department Head / Approver | Department Manager provides the equivalent review/clearance role. Confidential scope is absent. |
| Records Officer | Missing as a role and as archive/retention/disposal capability. |
| Auditor / Read-only User | No dedicated role. Permission grants can approximate read-only use of an existing role, but viewing scope and inherited focal/OIC grants need care. |

Role names are fixed; permission sets are configurable. OIC is separate from document assignee, so nominating an OIC does not permanently transfer ownership. General all-stage self-approval prevention and dated delegation are incomplete. Authority policy, privacy, retention and government compliance cannot be certified from this repository alone; the applicable agency rules were not supplied.

## Seven distinguishing features

| Feature | Status | Main remaining work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Complete document journey | Partial | Stored file versions, signature/delivery/archive stages. |
| Automatic deadline and escalation engine | Partial | Escalation, scheduled alerts, extensions/pauses. |

## Rollout phase assessment

| Suggested phase | Current position |
| --- | --- |
| 1 Foundation | Mostly operational; attachment storage, confidentiality and uniqueness/restore hardening remain. |
| 2 Workflow | Review/return/OIC/release operational; true file revision history, acknowledgment and dispatch delivery details absent. |
| 3 Monitoring | Deadlines/holidays/queues/analytics operational; active reminders, escalation and distinct role dashboards incomplete. |
| 4 Reporting | CSV/printing/date-period reporting and links exist; XLSX, saved report presets, bulk import and integration absent. |
| 5 Optimization | Designer UI is ahead of runtime; OCR/full-text/comparison/AI are absent, advanced process analysis partial. |

The app does not fit a single completed phase: it already has some later-phase features while earlier reliability/file-management requirements remain incomplete.

## Recommended architecture versus actual app

| Roadmap recommendation | Actual implementation / assessment |
| --- | --- |
| React + TypeScript | Vanilla JavaScript/CSS/HTML. This is an implementation difference, not evidence that the listed user features are missing. |
| Node.js API | Available using node:http and built-in modules. |
| PostgreSQL | SQLite using node:sqlite, with JSON operational records and in-memory state. No PostgreSQL/ORM. |
| Attachment/version file storage | Missing; external links only. |
| Automated backups/tested recovery | Daily local SQLite backups and JSON round trips exist; recovery completeness/validation gaps remain. |
| VPN/secure tunnel/remote access | Deployment-dependent and unverified. HTTPS reverse-proxy examples are provided; no VPN/tunnel service is implemented. |
| Self-hosted operation with optional integrations | Available. Single application process per database is the documented constraint. |
| Roughly 20–25 office users | No independent-user capacity certification. Existing benchmarks use up to twenty concurrent requests from one authenticated session. |

No React/PostgreSQL migration is necessary merely to tick user-facing features. If volumes grow, fix state synchronization, transaction consistency, schema validation and metadata indexing based on measured needs.

## Suggested gap priorities

These priorities are review recommendations for the current app, not a claim that the pasted roadmap assigned a priority to every bullet.

- P0: reject malformed restores before committing; enforce username and tracking-number uniqueness; make critical document/sequence/audit writes atomic; establish confidential/division viewing if required by agency policy; verify backups and document recovery.
- P1: file storage/version history and attachment gates; recipient acknowledgment; timed delegation and stronger separation of duties; active deadline reminders/escalation; outgoing proof/delivery; correct monthly release reporting and exact chart drill-down; old/new change audit; independent-user testing.
- P2: live workflow runtime with migration comparison; per-stage SLAs/pause/extension ledger; saved views/report templates; native XLSX; previewed validated bulk imports; retention/archive; OCR/full-text and version comparison; optional integrations.
- P3: semantic search, AI classification/extraction/summaries, predictive completion and anomaly models after authorization/data integrity are stable.

- Machine-readable item assessment: .test-runs/feature-audit-20261015/items.json.

The catalog totals deliberately retain repeated items and bundled requirements. Partial is not a completed checkbox, and Unverified is not evidence that a deployed control is absent.

# DTS partial features — 15 October 2026

A related feature exists, but one or more parts of the roadmap requirement are incomplete, absent, or defective.

**170 roadmap rows.** Repeated and overlapping requirements retain their original IDs; this is not a count of unique modules.

Full context: [Complete feature audit](<D:/test server/staging-dts/New folder/dts-final version-workflow/DTS-Final Version/FULL-FEATURE-AUDIT-2026-10-15.md>).

## Document intake and registration

| ID | Roadmap requirement | Existing behavior / remaining gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A2 | Automatically generate a unique tracking number. | Sequential prefix-year-counter numbers exist for ordinary documents; Special Report display codes omit schedule identity and can repeat. |
| A6 | Select document type, category, and subcategory. | Document and communication types and transaction classification exist; no category/subcategory taxonomy. |
| A7 | Record reference number and reference date. | Reference number exists; no dedicated reference-date field. |
| A9 | Record the source and method of receipt: physical, email, courier, or digital. | Source and communication type exist; receipt channel is not a separate configurable field. |
| A12 | Flag confidential, urgent, or restricted documents. | Normal/Urgent priority exists; no confidentiality or restricted-access classification. |
| A22 | Draft registration that can be completed later. | A valid received record can be saved without forwarding; there is no incomplete draft/autosave state. |
| A23 | Record linking for related documents, attachments, and previous submissions. | External document URLs exist; no related-record links or previous-submission relationship model. |

## Document tracking and lifecycle

| ID | Roadmap requirement | Existing behavior / remaining gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B1 | Unique tracking number for every document. | Ordinary documents have sequential DTS codes; Special Reports generated by different schedules can share SR-year-month labels. |
| B7 | Total processing time from receipt to closure. | Elapsed/current-stage and working-day turnaround are available; the principal turnaround KPI starts at assignment and stops at clearance, not receipt-to-final-release. |
| B10 | Reason for return, rejection, cancellation, or reassignment. | Return reasons are required and reassignment notes are supported; cancellation/rejection states and universally mandatory reassignment reasons are absent. |
| B14 | Archived, closed, cancelled, and reopened records. | Released records remain searchable; no archive/cancel/reopen lifecycle. |
| B16 | Viewable movement receipt for every handoff. | Printable routing slip contains movements; there is no separate acknowledged receipt per handoff. |

| S4 | Automatically notify the next responsible person after forwarding. | The next actor's queue/bell updates; no persistent event notification or delivery. |
| S9 | Automatically generate transmittal sheets. | Routing slips are generated on demand; no automatic transmittal numbering/sheet job. |
| S12 | Automatically remind users about documents awaiting their action. | Queue and due-soon indicators exist; no active scheduled reminders. |
| S14 | Automatically flag unusual inactivity or repeated returns. | Admin/Focal stalled thresholds and revision cycle counts exist; no generalized inactivity/repeated-return anomaly rules. |
| S15 | Provide configurable rules for exceptional situations. | OIC/override/resubmission/reference rules exist; designer conditions do not execute production workflows. |

## Collaboration and team coordination

| ID | Roadmap requirement | Existing behavior / remaining gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| T1 | Internal discussion threads attached to documents. | Timestamped notes and reviewer comments exist; no nested threaded discussions. |
| T3 | Private internal notes separated from official correspondence. | Notes are separate from the external file, but have no private visibility tier. |
| T4 | Shared task lists. | Staff/workload views share document action lists; no independent shared task-list objects. |
| T6 | Staff availability and temporary unavailability. | Manager present/away flags and personnel leave calendar exist; staff leave is not automatically integrated into routing availability. |
| T7 | Delegation during leave or official travel. | Manual manager/OIC delegation exists; no general timed staff substitution linked to leave. |
| T11 | Handover notes when an employee leaves or changes assignment. | Reassignment accepts a note; no structured employee departure/handover record. |
| T12 | Activity feed showing recent team actions. | Authorized audit/history can show actions; no separate recent team activity feed. |

## Integrations

| ID | Roadmap requirement | Existing behavior / remaining gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| U1 | Google Drive links or API integration. | Drive URLs can be stored; no authenticated Drive API integration. |
| U10 | Calendar integration for deadlines and reminders. | Personnel calendar is implemented; document-deadline calendar/sync/reminders are absent. |
| U12 | Internal API for future agency systems. | Internal cookie-authenticated APIs exist; no published service API/auth contract for other agency systems. |
| U15 | Export to existing departmental reporting templates. | Generic reports/routing slips exist; no agency template import/mapping/export integration. |

## Advanced and experimental features

| ID | Roadmap requirement | Existing behavior / remaining gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| V4 | Bottleneck and workload analysis. | Workload, desk-age and return-cycle indicators exist; no historical process mining or automatic workload balancing. |
| V10 | Anomaly detection. | Simple Admin/Focal stall thresholds exist; no statistical/anomaly model. |

## Performance and technical reliability

| ID | Roadmap requirement | Existing behavior / remaining gap |
| --- | --- | --- |
| W1 | Indexed database columns for common searches. | IDs and workflow/report lookup indexes exist; ordinary searchable document metadata is JSON and is searched in browser memory. |
| W3 | Transactions for critical workflow operations. | Graph/report/restore operations use SQL transactions; ordinary document mutation, sequence and audit writes are not one combined transaction. |
| W4 | Prevention of duplicate submissions and conflicting updates. | Workflow drafts have revision conflict checks and recurring reports have unique keys; ordinary updates lack general revision/idempotency protection and user creation has a confirmed race. |
| W5 | Database constraints for important relationships. | Workflow foreign keys and recurring-report uniqueness exist; many user/document/division references inside JSON have no database constraints. |
| W6 | Background jobs for scheduled reports and bulk processing. | In-process timers generate recurring reports; no durable worker queue or bulk-processing job system. |
| W7 | Centralized error logging. | Console error logging exists; no centralized persistent error service/view. |
| W8 | Health checks and uptime monitoring. | healthz exists; no uptime monitor/alerts. |
| W11 | Database migration versioning. | CREATE IF NOT EXISTS and compatibility upgrades exist; no numbered migration ledger. |
| W13 | Concurrent-user testing. | Concurrent requests are tested; independent simultaneous user-session soak testing is absent. |
| W14 | Backup restoration testing. | JSON restore round trips are tested; full snapshot disaster recovery and malformed restore rejection are incomplete. |
| W16 | Separation of development, testing, and production environments. | DATA_DIR and isolated test runner separate databases; no formal development/staging/production deployment configuration. |

async function api(method,url,body,opts){
 opts=opts||{};let res;
 try{res=await fetch(url,{method:method,credentials:'same-origin',headers:Object.assign({'X-Requested-With':'dts'},body!==undefined?{'Content-Type':'application/json'}:{},opts.poll?{'X-Poll':'1'}:{}),body:body!==undefined?JSON.stringify(body):undefined});}
 catch(e){return{ok:false,status:0,error:'Cannot reach the server. Check your connection and try again.'};}
 let data={};try{data=await res.json();}catch(e){}
 if(data.orgName)UI.orgName=data.orgName;
 const out=Object.assign({},data,{ok:res.ok&&data.ok!==false,status:res.status});
 if(res.status===401&&!opts.noAuthRedirect){sessionEnded(data.error);return out;}
 if(res.status===403&&data.code==='must_change_password'){forcePasswordChange();return out;}
 return out;
}

 S=r.state;S.audit=[];API.v=r.v;rebuildHS();SESSION.uid=r.me;render();return true;
}

function myQueue(u){return visibleDocs(u).filter(d=>isMyTurn(u,d));}
const actionDocs=myQueue; // "For My Action" is exactly: every document where it is this user's turn to act (isMyTurn), for every role and every stage. No stage/role special-casing here — that was the root cause of the previous bug (assignees, dept managers and admin staff were excluded from this list even though isMyTurn already correctly recognised their turn).

 const u=ME(),nav=navItems(u),q=myQueue(u),spN=spMine(u).length;
 const link=n=>'<a href="#/'+n[0]+'" data-act="nav" data-r="'+n[0]+'"'+((UI.route===n[0]||(UI.route==='doc'&&n[0]==='dashboard'))?' aria-current="page"':'')+'>'+ic(n[2])+'<span>'+esc(n[1])+'</span>'+(n[0]==='action'&&(q.length+spN)?'<span class="cnt" title="Waiting for your action">'+(q.length+spN)+'</span>':'')+'</a>';
 return '<div class="shell"><aside class="side'+(UI.side?' open':'')+'" aria-label="Main navigation"><div class="brand">'+LOGO_WHITE+'<div><b>Document Tracking</b><span>&amp; Routing System</span></div></div>'+
 '<nav class="nav"><h4>Workspace</h4>'+nav.w.map(link).join('')+(nav.a.length?'<h4>Administration</h4>'+nav.a.map(link).join(''):'')+'</nav>'+
 '<div class="sidefoot"><div class="nm">'+esc(u.name)+'</div><div>'+esc(roleName(u.role))+(isOIC(u)?' · acting as OIC':'')+'</div></div></aside><div class="scrim'+(UI.side?' on':'')+'" data-act="closeSide"></div>'+
 '<div class="main"><header class="top"><button class="iconbtn burger" data-act="openSide" aria-label="Open menu">'+ic('menu')+'</button>'+AppLogo('topLogo')+
 '<form class="search" data-submit="topSearch" role="search"><span aria-hidden="true">'+ic('search')+'</span><input name="q" placeholder="Find by tracking no., subject or sender" aria-label="Search documents"></form><div class="grow"></div>'+
 (has(u,'doc.log')?'<button class="btn primary" data-act="newDoc">'+ic('plus')+'<span class="hide-md">Log document</span></button>':'')+
 '<button class="iconbtn" data-act="shortcuts" aria-label="Keyboard shortcuts" title="Keyboard shortcuts (?)">'+ic('help')+'</button>'+'<div class="menu-wrap"><button class="iconbtn" data-act="menu" data-m="bell" aria-label="Documents waiting for you" aria-expanded="'+(UI.menu==='bell')+'">'+ic('bell')+(q.length?'<span class="dotn">'+q.length+'</span>':'')+'</button>'+(UI.menu==='bell'?bellMenu(u,q):'')+'</div>'+
 '<div class="menu-wrap"><button class="avatar" data-act="menu" data-m="user" aria-label="Account menu" aria-expanded="'+(UI.menu==='user')+'">'+esc(initials(u.name))+'</button>'+(UI.menu==='user'?userMenu(u):'')+'</div></header>'+
 '<main class="view" id="view" tabindex="-1"></main></div></div>';
}
function bellMenu(u,q){
 const top=q.slice().sort((a,b)=>({over:0,soon:1}[dl(a).key]??2)-({over:0,soon:1}[dl(b).key]??2)).slice(0,6);
 return '<div class="menu" role="menu"><div class="mh">Waiting for your action ('+q.length+')</div>'+(top.length?top.map(d=>'<a class="mi" href="#/doc/'+d.id+'" data-act="openDoc" data-id="'+d.id+'"><b class="mono small">'+esc(d.trackNo)+'</b> '+esc(d.subject.slice(0,60))+'<small>'+esc(STAGES[d.status].short)+' – '+esc(dl(d).label)+'</small></a>').join(''):(spMine(u).length?'':'<div class="empty">Nothing is waiting on you.</div>'))+
 (spMine(u).length?'<a class="mi" href="#/action" data-act="nav" data-r="action"><b>'+plural(spMine(u).length,'Special Report')+'</b> for your action<small>Open For My Action</small></a>':'')+
 (q.length?'<button class="mi b" data-act="myQueue">See all in My queue</button>':'')+'</div>';
}
function userMenu(u){
 const away=(u.role==='dept_manager'||u.role==='div_manager');
 return '<div class="menu" role="menu"><div class="mh">'+esc(u.name)+'<div class="small muted">'+esc(roleName(u.role))+(u.division?' – '+esc(u.division):'')+'</div></div>'+
 '<button class="mi" data-act="myAccount">My account<small>Change your password</small></button>'+
 (away?'<button class="mi" data-act="togglePresence" data-id="'+u.id+'">'+(u.present?'Mark myself as away':'Mark myself as present')+'<small>'+(u.present?'You are shown as present':'Reviews route to your alternate while you are away')+'</small></button>':'')+
 '<div class="mi" style="cursor:default"><span class="lbl">Theme</span><div class="row" style="margin-top:6px">'+['auto','light','dark'].map(t=>'<button class="btn sm'+(UI.theme===t?' primary':'')+'" data-act="theme" data-t="'+t+'">'+t[0].toUpperCase()+t.slice(1)+'</button>').join('')+'</div></div>'+
 '<button class="mi" data-act="signout">Sign out</button></div>';
}
function applyTheme(){const r=document.documentElement;if(UI.theme==='auto')r.removeAttribute('data-theme');else r.setAttribute('data-theme',UI.theme);}

function render(){
 const old=$('#view'),key=UI.route+'/'+UI.param,st=(old&&UI._key===key)?old.scrollTop:0;
 if(!ME()){$('#app').innerHTML=loginHTML();UI._key='';return;}
 $('#app').innerHTML=shellHTML();
 const v=$('#view');
 const okf=ROUTE_OK[UI.route];
 let h;
 try{
  if(!okf)h=VIEWS.notfound(ME());
  else if(!okf(ME()))h='<div class="wrap"><div class="card empty"><b>You do not have access to this page.</b>Ask the System Administrator if your role should include it.</div></div>';
  else h=VIEWS[UI.route](ME(),UI.param);
 }catch(e){console.error(e);h='<div class="wrap"><div class="card empty"><b>This page could not be displayed.</b>'+esc(e.message)+'</div></div>';}
 v.innerHTML=h;v.scrollTop=st;UI._key=key;
 if(UI.route==='dashboard')refreshReg();
 markSeen();
 if(UI.route==='audit'&&ME())setTimeout(loadAudit,0);
}
const VIEWS={notfound:()=>'<div class="wrap"><div class="card empty"><b>Page not found.</b><a href="#/dashboard" data-act="nav" data-r="dashboard">Back to the dashboard</a></div></div>'};

/* =====================  GLOBAL EVENTS  ===================== */
const ACTIONS={},CHANGES={},INPUTS={};
document.addEventListener('click',ev=>{
 UI.lastAct=Date.now();
 const t=ev.target.closest('[data-act]');
 if(UI.menu&&!ev.target.closest('.menu-wrap')){UI.menu=null;$$('.menu').forEach(m=>m.remove());}
 if(!t)return;
 const a=t.dataset.act;
 if(a==='modalBack'&&ev.target!==t)return;
 const fn=ACTIONS[a];
 if(fn){if(t.tagName==='A')ev.preventDefault();fn(t,ev);}
});
document.addEventListener('change',ev=>{const t=ev.target.closest('[data-chg]');if(t&&CHANGES[t.dataset.chg])CHANGES[t.dataset.chg](t,ev);});
document.addEventListener('input',ev=>{const t=ev.target.closest('[data-inp]');if(t&&INPUTS[t.dataset.inp])INPUTS[t.dataset.inp](t,ev);});
document.addEventListener('submit',ev=>{const f=ev.target.closest('[data-submit]');if(f){ev.preventDefault();if(ACTIONS[f.dataset.submit])ACTIONS[f.dataset.submit](f,ev);}});
document.addEventListener('keydown',ev=>{UI.lastAct=Date.now();if(ev.key==='Escape'){if($('#pp.on'))closePP();else if($('.modal-back')&&!$('.modal-back[data-locked]'))closeModal();else if(UI.menu){UI.menu=null;$$('.menu').forEach(m=>m.remove());}}});

ACTIONS.nav=(t)=>go(t.dataset.r);
ACTIONS.openDoc=(t)=>go('doc',t.dataset.id);
ACTIONS.openSide=()=>{UI.side=true;render();};
ACTIONS.closeSide=()=>{UI.side=false;render();};
ACTIONS.closeModal=()=>closeModal();
ACTIONS.modalBack=()=>closeModal();
ACTIONS.confirmYes=()=>{const f=UI.confirmFn;closeModal();UI.confirmFn=null;if(f)f();};
ACTIONS.ppClose=()=>closePP();
ACTIONS.ppPrint=()=>{try{window.print();}catch(e){toast('Use Ctrl/Cmd + P to print this preview.');}};
ACTIONS.menu=(t)=>{UI.menu=UI.menu===t.dataset.m?null:t.dataset.m;render();};
ACTIONS.theme=(t)=>{UI.theme=t.dataset.t;tSet(UI.theme);applyTheme();render();};
ACTIONS.signout=()=>signOut('');
ACTIONS.myQueue=()=>{UI.filters={tile:'action'};UI.page=1;UI.sel.clear();go('dashboard');};
CHANGES.showpw=(t)=>{$('#lp').type=t.checked?'text':'password';};
ACTIONS.login=async(f)=>{
 const v=formVals(f),e=$('#lerr'),btn=$('button[type=submit]',f);
 btn.disabled=true;
 const r=await api('POST','/api/login',{username:v.username,password:v.password,remember:!!v.remember},{noAuthRedirect:true});
 btn.disabled=false;
 if(!r.ok){e.hidden=false;e.innerHTML=ic('alert')+'<div>'+esc(r.error||'Could not sign in.')+'</div>';return;}
 UI.notice='';UI.lastAct=Date.now();parseHash();await refresh();
};
ACTIONS.topSearch=(f)=>{const q=formVals(f).q.trim();UI.filters={tile:'total',q:q};UI.page=1;UI.sel.clear();go('dashboard');};
ACTIONS.togglePresence=async(t)=>{
 const u=uById(t.dataset.id);if(!u)return;UI.menu=null;
 const r=await api('POST','/api/users/'+u.id+'/presence',{present:!u.present});
 if(!r.ok)return toast(r.error,'bad');
 toast(u.name+' is now shown as '+(u.present?'away':'present')+'.');await refresh();
};
ACTIONS.myAccount=()=>{UI.menu=null;const u=ME();openModal('My account',
 '<dl class="dl" style="margin-bottom:16px"><dt>Name</dt><dd>'+esc(u.name)+'</dd><dt>Username</dt><dd class="mono">'+esc(u.username||'')+'</dd><dt>Role</dt><dd>'+esc(roleName(u.role))+'</dd><dt>Last sign-in</dt><dd>'+fmtDT(u.lastLogin)+'</dd></dl><h3 style="margin-bottom:8px">Change password</h3><div data-form="pw"><div class="fld"><label>Current password</label><input type="password" name="cur" autocomplete="current-password"></div><div class="g2"><div class="fld"><label>New password</label><input type="password" name="n1" autocomplete="new-password"></div><div class="fld"><label>Confirm new password</label><input type="password" name="n2" autocomplete="new-password"></div></div><p class="hint">Use at least 8 characters with a mix of letters and numbers.</p></div>',
 {foot:'<button class="btn" data-act="closeModal">Close</button><button class="btn primary" data-act="savePw">Save password</button>'});};
async function changePw(sel,done){
 const v=formVals($(sel));
 const pe=pwProblem(v.n1);if(pe)return toast(pe,'bad');if(v.n1!==v.n2)return toast('The new passwords do not match.','bad');
 const r=await api('POST','/api/password',{current:v.cur,next:v.n1},{noAuthRedirect:true});
 if(!r.ok)return toast(r.error||'Could not change the password.','bad');
 closeModal();toast('Password updated.');await done();
}
ACTIONS.savePw=()=>changePw('[data-form=pw]',()=>refresh());
ACTIONS.saveForcedPw=()=>changePw('[data-form=fpw]',()=>refresh());
function pwProblem(p){if(!p||p.length<8)return 'The password must have at least 8 characters.';if(!/[A-Za-z]/.test(p)||!/[0-9]/.test(p))return 'The password needs both letters and numbers.';return '';}

/* idle sign-out and live updates from other users */
setInterval(()=>{const m=S&&S.settings?S.settings.idleMinutes:0;if(ME()&&m>0&&Date.now()-UI.lastAct>m*60000)signOut('You were signed out after '+m+' minutes of inactivity.');},20000);
(()=>{
 let tip=null;
 document.addEventListener('mouseover',ev=>{
  const a=ev.target.closest('[data-preview]');
  if(!a)return;
  if(tip)tip.remove();
  const url=a.dataset.preview;
  const t=document.createElement('div');
  t.className='url-tip';
  t.innerHTML='<div class="url-tip-inner"><div class="url-tip-bar">'+ic('send').replace('<svg','<svg width="14" height="14"')+'<span>'+esc(url.replace(/^https?:\/\//,'').slice(0,80))+'</span></div><iframe src="'+esc(url)+'" sandbox="allow-same-origin" title="URL preview" loading="lazy"></iframe></div>';
  document.body.appendChild(t);
  tip=t;
  const r=a.getBoundingClientRect();
  t.style.cssText='position:fixed;z-index:200;left:'+Math.min(r.left,window.innerWidth-320)+'px;top:'+(r.bottom+8)+'px';
 },true);
 document.addEventListener('mouseout',ev=>{
  const a=ev.target.closest('[data-preview]');
  if(!a)return;
  const to=ev.relatedTarget;if(to&&(a.contains(to)||tip&&tip.contains(to)))return;
  if(tip){tip.remove();tip=null;}
 },true);
})();

function typing(){const a=document.activeElement;return !!a&&/^(INPUT|TEXTAREA|SELECT)$/.test(a.tagName)&&(a.type==='checkbox'||a.type==='radio'?false:(a.value||'').length>0);}
setInterval(async()=>{
 if(!ME()||document.hidden)return;
 const r=await api('GET','/api/version',undefined,{poll:true,noAuthRedirect:true});
 if(r.status===401){sessionEnded('Your session has ended. Please sign in again.');return;}
 if(r.ok&&r.v!==API.v){if($('#modalRoot').innerHTML||$('#pp.on')||typing())return;await refresh(true);}
},15000);

/* Broaden what counts as activity: click and keydown already update UI.lastAct (see the
   delegated listeners above); mouse movement, scrolling and touch are added here so a
   person reading or scrolling without clicking is still correctly treated as active. */
['mousemove','scroll','touchstart','pointerdown'].forEach(evName=>document.addEventListener(evName,()=>{UI.lastAct=Date.now();},{passive:true}));

/* Auto-refresh after 60 seconds of no activity. This re-fetches state and re-renders the
   CURRENT route in place (render() never resets UI.route/UI.filters/UI.page/UI.sel/UI.sort),
   so the active tab, filters, search text, pagination and selected rows all survive exactly
   as if nothing happened — there is no separate reload/navigation. The existing signed-in
   session is reused via the same cookie; if the server has separately timed out the session
   (see the idle sign-out above, which uses a much longer default), refresh() already handles
   that by returning to the sign-in screen, same as it does for the 15-second poll above. */
let lastIdleRefresh=0;
setInterval(()=>{
 if(!ME()||document.hidden)return;
 const idleFor=Date.now()-UI.lastAct;
 if(idleFor<60000)return;
 if(Date.now()-lastIdleRefresh<60000)return;
 if($('#modalRoot').innerHTML||$('#pp.on')||typing())return;
 lastIdleRefresh=Date.now();
 refresh(true);
},5000);

 inbox:'<path d="M22 12h-6l-2 3h-4l-2-3H2"/><path d="M5.5 5.1L2 12v6a2 2 0 0 0 2 2h16a2 2 0 0 0 2-2v-6l-3.5-6.9A2 2 0 0 0 16.7 4H7.3a2 2 0 0 0-1.8 1.1z"/>',
 alertc:'<circle cx="12" cy="12" r="9"/><path d="M12 8v5M12 16.5h.01"/>',

/* =====================  LIFECYCLE PROGRESS  ===================== */
const LC_STEPS=[{l:'Logged'},{l:'Focal'},{l:'Staff'},{l:'Supervisor'},{l:'Division'},{l:'Dept. Mgr'},{l:'Cleared'},{l:'Out'}];
const LC_IDX={received:0,at_focal:1,processing:2,compliance:2,sup_review:3,div_review:4,dept_review:5,cleared:6,return_out:6,out:7};
const LEVEL_SHORT={sup:'Supervisor',div:'Division',dept:'Dept. Mgr'};
const ago=t=>{const m=Math.floor((Date.now()-new Date(t))/60000);if(m<1)return 'just now';if(m<60)return m+' min ago';const h=Math.floor(m/60);if(h<24)return h+' h ago';const dd=Math.floor(h/24);return dd+' day'+(dd>1?'s':'')+' ago';};
function lcInfo(d){
 const idx=LC_IDX[d.status],last=LC_STEPS.length-1,over=isClockActive(d)&&dl(d).key==='over';
 let chip='',tone='neu',ring=over?'bad':'warn';
 switch(d.status){
  case 'received':chip='Awaiting forwarding';tone='neu';break;
  case 'at_focal':chip='Awaiting assignment';tone='info';break;
  case 'processing':chip=d.autoRouted&&!d.cycles?'With designated staff':'With staff';tone='info';break;
  case 'compliance':{const oc=openComments(d),lv=oc.length?oc[oc.length-1].level:d.returnedFrom;chip=(LEVEL_SHORT[lv]||'Reviewer')+' comment: comply';tone='pur';ring=over?'bad':'pur';break;}
  case 'sup_review':chip='In supervisor review';tone='info';break;
  case 'div_review':chip='In division review';tone='info';break;
  case 'dept_review':chip='Final review';tone='info';break;
  case 'cleared':chip='Cleared for out';tone='ok';ring='ok';break;
  case 'return_out':chip='For return: to log out';tone='warn';ring='warn';break;
  case 'out':chip=d.outType==='For return'?'Returned to sender':'Dispatched';tone=d.outType==='For return'?'warn':'ok';ring='ok';break;
 }
